ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-09.u184	It's a résumé. Dear Mr. Chairman, honourable Members! The 76th sentence of the Rules of Organization and Procedure of the National Assembly should be presented in summary, as you have agreed. ♪ I can't believe for the first vote on the Draft Budget Bill of the National Health Insurance Fund for 2022, No 47-202-01-8, submitted by the Council of Ministers on 2 February 2022. At its extraordinary meeting held on 4 February 2022, the Health Committee examined and discussed the Draft Budget Bill of the National Health Insurance Fund for 2022, No 47-202-01-8, submitted by the Council of Ministers on 2 February 2022. The meeting was attended by the Minister of Health Professor Asena Serbenova; the Chairman of the Supervisory Board of the National Health Insurance Fund Associate Professor Vasil Pandov; the Governor of NHIF Dr. Yordanka Penkova; representatives of the Ministry of Health, word organizations in the field of health care, trade unions and patient organizations. The draft and the reasons for the draft were presented by the Chairman of the Supervisory Board of the NHIF Associate Professor Vasil Pandov, who briefed the MPs on the main financial parameters laid down in the draft budget of the National Health Insurance Fund for 2022. The planned revenues and transfers amount to BGN 6 050 749 900. The funds considered shall be in the event of the admission of a health insurance contribution of 8 per cent, reflecting the effect of an increase in the minimum social security income for self-employed persons and the effect on the amount of health insurance transfers from an increase in the part of the insurance income on which the State contributes health contributions to the persons insured at the expense of the State budget. The employer and the health insured person's pay ratio for 2022 is 60:40. The provided costs and transfers amount to BGN 6 050 749 900, of which BGN 5 678 270 100. they're for health insurance payments. The planned funds provide for the purchase of medical assistance within the scope of compulsory health insurance guaranteed by the NHIF budget. Prof. Pandov stressed that in 2022 the funds for health insurance payments for primary outpatient medical care (PMP) are increased, specialized outpatient medical care (SRM), medical diagnostic activity (MID), hospital medical care (BMP), dental aid (DP) and medicinal products, medical devices and dietary foods for special medical purposes. He stressed that the significant increase in funds for the IPMP, SIM and IDA is in line with the government's policy to strengthen the role of prevention as an opportunity for better health and quality of life of Bulgarian citizens. The reserve, including for unforeseen and urgent expenses, according to art. 26, para. 1 of the Health Insurance Act (Health Insurance Act) is three percent of the collected income from health insurance contributions and transfers for health insurance from other budgets and is worth 174 075 500 BGN. The order of Art. 5 of the bill is in response to the need to continue providing the possibility by means of transfer from the central budget for the payment of a permanent monthly supplement to the remuneration of medical staff in the conditions and for the period of declared exceptional epidemic situation under art. 61, para. 1 of the Health Act. The provision of additional remuneration aims at stimulating and supporting medical staff in medical institutions, who works under enormous tensions in an emergency epidemic situation and daily performs activities related to the diagnosis, treatment and monitoring of patients with CIVID-19 infection, as well as for the management of the pandemic and its consequences. In order to achieve a level playing field, it is proposed in 2022 that those working in hospital pharmacies should be included as a separate category of entitled persons to receive funds under art. 5 of the Law. The proposed establishment of a new group of medicinal products compared to the three existing groups in the ZNZIF for 2021 is the result of an established need to differentiate a group of medicinal products that are of extremely high value and which have a significant impact on the costs of NHIF. The proposal concerns medicinal products that are used for the treatment of rare and severe diseases and for a more limited group of patients. This group includes innovative, high-tech medicinal products, targeted therapies and medicinal products intended for personalized treatment. It should be borne in mind that the new group includes medicinal products for the treatment of less than 1% of health insured persons and the reimbursement for them represents more than one third of all the costs of medicinal products under the NHIF budget. The separation of these medicinal products and the separation of costs for them from the other groups will lead, on the one hand, to the possibility for the NHIF to provide the necessary costly treatment to the patients in need and, on the other, to a fairer application of the burden of the mechanism of predictability and sustainability of the NHIF budget to all participating holders of marketing authorisations. In this way, the pressure on budgets will be reduced for other groups of medicinal products that provide basic and established therapies for socially significant diseases affecting a large proportion of health insured persons and for which the costs of the NHIF are significantly below the costs of medicinal products proposed to be divided into a separate group. The transitional and final provisions provide for amendments and additions to the Health Insurance Act and the Health Act. In accordance with the current terminology relating to prescription of medicinal products, a change in art. 24, p. 9 of the SCI and in art. 45, para. 24, as prescription forms are replaced by electronic prescriptions. The amendment proposal to art. 45, para. 29 of the ICA aims to ensure the appropriate expenditure of the NHIF budget, which is the main financial plan for raising and spending the cash of compulsory health insurance, and is related to providing timely information between the institutions for decisions issued and entered into force, which has an effect on the expenditure of the NHIF budget. The proposal to change the existing provision of art. 45, para. 32 of the ICA provides for the mechanism ensuring predictability and sustainability of the NHIF budget to be a regulatory administrative act. The proposal is the result of the need to clarify its legal nature, its subject matter and scope, the parties and the methodology for its application. In order to avoid different interpretations in judicial disputes, the proposal is aimed at legal regulation of the legal nature of the mechanism. With the change in art. 82, para. 6 of the Health Act lays down a special one-month period for the decision of the authority of a party in the proceedings for approval or refusal of payment of medical and other services outside the scope of compulsory health insurance. In conclusion, Associate Professor Pandov pointed out that the expected results planned to be achieved in 2022 within the framework of the funds for health insurance payments laid down in the budget for 2022 are: ensuring the effective functioning of the compulsory health insurance system, ensuring that health insured persons are provided with affordable, quality and timely medical assistance, as well as ensuring equal access of healthcare institutions to the compulsory health insurance system in compliance with the requirements of the legislation in force. In the discussion were presented opinions from the Bulgarian Medical Union, the Bulgarian Dental Union, the Bulgarian Pharmaceutical Union, the Bulgarian Association of Assistant Pharmacists, the Bulgarian Dental Association, the National Association of General Practitioners in Bulgaria, the National Association of Municipalities in the Republic of Bulgaria, the Confederation of Independent Trade Unions in Bulgaria, the Syndicate of Administrative Employees During the discussion, the members of the Commission discussed the financial parameters proposed in the Bill and expressed principled support for the opinions presented by the word organizations, trade unions and NGOs. Specific questions were raised regarding the possibility for the NHIF to ensure the provision of accessible and quality medical assistance to all health-insured Bulgarian citizens within the framework of the proposed budget for 2022. It has been pointed out that increasing the financial resource in itself will not lead to a significant change in the functioning of our health insurance system without the introduction of clear policies and mechanisms for adequate distribution and spending of the funds from the budget of the National Health Insurance Fund. It was stressed that between the first and second votes of the Bill, the main problems should be discussed and the opportunities for their resolution for the benefit of all Bulgarian citizens and those involved in the healthcare system should be sought. On the basis of the discussion and the vote carried out on the following results: the draft budget of the National Health Insurance Fund for 2022, No 47-202-01-8, submitted by the Council of Ministers on 2 February 2022.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-09.u348	Thank you. . - "Employment accident and occupational disease" Fund. An surplus of 198,0 million. lv. - Total disease and disability fund lv. - "Unemployment" fund "Lack of 172,0 million." lv. - NSI's budget - surplus of 6 661.1 million lv. The total result of the Funds and the NSI budget shall be balanced to zero. The transitional and final provisions of the Bill are related to the implementation of the budget of state social security. The bill meets the requirements of the Law on Legislative Acts and the Decree on its implementation, as well as of the Rules on Organization and Activity of the National Assembly. A discussion was held on the bill, which included the following opinions and requests: MPs from the parliamentary group of GERB SDS Kiril Ananiev, Alexander Ivanov, Krasimir Valchev and Irena Dimova spoke about the failure to support the bill by expressing concerns about: - the continuation of the trend for the gradual increase of the share of funds in the Fund, which is collected from direct transfers from the central budget at the expense of the reduction of the share of the contributions; - the increase of the minimum insurance income of registered farmers and tobacco producers from 420 BGN. £710. and increase the maximum insurance income of 3000 BGN. At 3,400 leva. from 1 April 2022, which leads to an increase in the tax burden and can lead to less collection, as Bulgarian citizens working in sectors with greater added value, farmers with low incomes, respectively, may try not to provide their real income. It should be borne in mind that information technology and artificial intelligence are the sector with the greatest increase in added value and in the future it will set the line between developed and developing countries. In addition, the 410 million bets. lv. the measures 60/40, 75/0 are extremely insufficient. Concerns were also expressed as to whether it was better to wait for the pandemic to pass so that small farmers could have this difficult year. Similar concerns about the excessive increase of the minimum insurance income by 290 BGN. also expressed by the People's Representative Bajram Bajram from the Movement for Rights and Freedoms. According to him, the increase should be smooth and not abrupt, as there is currently a type of crisis, inflation, which leads to an increase in feed prices and everything else that will make it difficult for farmers. The opposite opinion was advocated by the people's representative Rumen Gechev from the BSP for Bulgaria and the Deputy Prime Minister and Minister of Finance Asen Vassilev, who pointed out in their exhibition that people with high incomes are not only IT specialists, but also bankers, investment firms, and this increase is not enough to change their behaviour. On the contrary, it is more important for this group of people to have normal health care, education, good roads, responsive and competent state administration, the state to ensure their security and safety. In addition, the principle is that the minimum wage and maximum social income must move in parallel with the average wage. The increase of the average salary from the moment the threshold of 3000 BGN was introduced. so far, it is almost 20%, while the current increase in maximum social security income is 13%, that is, it is not ahead, but is slower than the increase in average wage. On the other hand, it is fair when they pay a higher amount of contribution and receive a greater pension in the future. In addition, it is extremely vicious practice that a sector in the country's economy, such as agriculture, has a minimum wage of 410 BGN. under the line of poverty - BGN 413, and this is not about an amount, as this amount as part of the budget of the NSI will not affect the overall picture. This is about an honest and unified approach to all those working in the country. If farmers and tobacco producers are having difficulties, they should be supported by the Ministry of Agriculture and not through a policy on minimum budget income. In support of these reasoning, the manager of NOI Ivaylo Ivanov pointed out that the number of persons with taxable income over 3000 BGN. is 217 000 and the number of persons with a taxable income above BGN 3400. is around 163 000, structurally with the largest share of 25% are information technologies, 11% healthcare, 6% government, 4.2% education and science and consulting business, 2,5% financial sector and so on. Therefore, this is not a huge number to influence the budget of state social security and to rely mainly on these funds. People's Representative Venko Sabrutev from "We are continuing the Change" was of the opinion that although there is a type of crisis, this is the right time to raise the insurance threshold as there is growth in the production, trade and consumption of food products. In support of the Bill, although with some nuances, expressed the people's representative Georgi Ganev from "Democratic Bulgaria", who stressed that this draft budget of state social security is making a serious attempt to introduce certain rules that do not depend on party-political communctures, but at the same time it is against the increase of the insurance burden, as the relationship between the personal contribution and how much pension is received is quite loose in the Bulgarian solidarity pension system. According to the President of BESKO Dobromir Ivanov, the problem of raising the maximum insurance income, on the one hand, is rather communicative, i.e. the lack of dialogue between the government and the industry, which creates high added value on the other, the lack of predictability. In addition, Ognian Troyanov, a BAIT representative, pointed out that this measure was taken at an inappropriate time when Western economies through the state and financial sector stimulate robotisation and automation. Because of the negative emotions created in the industry there is a real risk that many employees will come out of the main insurance system of the state. The representatives of the trade union organizations Asya Goneva from the CNSB and Vanya Grigorova from KT "Supporta" expressed in support of the budget of the state social security for 2022, but noted that once again the minimum insurance income is frozen, which fuels the grey economy and undeclared work. They explicitly pointed out that they do not have any information on what is being paid from the second pillar, which is why estimates cannot be made for the amount of payments for pensioners who receive funds under the poverty line. The employers' organizations in the face of Vasil Velev from AICB and Maria Mincheva from BIA voiced fears that with the increase of the compensation in the second year of maternity and the minimum and maximum unemployment compensation will be achieved for people to work. By its opinion, the Fiscal Council of the Republic of Bulgaria agreed the draft consolidated budget of the DVO for 2022 and concluded the following conclusions: the expenditure of the DVO budget increased by an additional 8.3% compared to the updated budget for 2021 and the increase in insurance revenues was 7.4%. The slowing rate of revenue growth compared to expenditure continues to deepen the deficit in the DVO budget. The planning of social costs should be carried out precisely and conservatively, in so far as the accelerated rate of growth in nominal terms would have a pro-inflation effect. The increase in the transfer from the state budget to cover the deficit of the DVO budget for 2022 increases the dependence of the social security system on back-financing through tax revenue, which poses a risk to the long-term financial sustainability of the pension system. The Ombudsman of the Republic of Bulgaria commented on the main problems in the pension system of 25 December 2021, proposing to discuss the possibility before updating the pensions of 1 July 2022 to be indexed with the percentages of 3.8 for 2018 and 5.7 for 2019; to review the action of art. 102, para. 2 of the CSR, on the official recalculation of pensions; to argue the ceiling of pensions. In addition, the lack of clarity was noted in determining the increase in maximum insurance income and the lack of a formula for determining the minimum wage. The Ombudsman expressed hope that the MPs would comply with the arguments put forward in the opinion and would not create a prerequisite for violation of civil rights. The written opinions of the social partners, in addition to support for certain activities, also express reservations about certain policies. The Confederation of Independent Trade Unions in Bulgaria assesses as short-sighted and not contributing to the long-term financial stability of the system the lack of growth in minimum insurance incomes in economic activities and groups of professions outside the application of the new minimum wage; the absence of even minimal increase in insurance contributions for the most deficit funds of the DEA; the lack of proposals for regulatory amendments that will dramatically improve insurance discipline. The CST does not accept as sufficiently justified the continued stagnation of the maximum pension earned by those insured with the highest contribution to the provision. The Confederation of Labour "Support" draws attention that 82.5% of economic activities have a minimum insurance income equal to the minimum wage, which contributes to the expansion of the grey economy and undeclared employment and enables unscrupulous employers to save from insurance, depriving their employees in the future. The Confederation of Employers and Industrialists in Bulgaria does not support the Bill and in particular does not support the increase of the minimum wage of 650 BGN. £710.; the increase of maximum insurance income from 3000 BGN At 3,400 leva.; increasing the minimum insurance income of self-insuring persons; increasing the minimum insurance income by basic economic activities and qualification groups. CRIB insists on the cancellation of payment at the expense of the employer on the first three days of the hospital and the dropping of additional remuneration for acquired work experience and professional experience, i.e. a class of time served. The Bulgarian Chamber of Commerce and Industry further raises the issue of removing the ceiling of pensions and returning to equal treatment of the burden of insurance for civil servants and employees in the private sector. The Bulgarian Industrial Association refrains from supporting the draft budget of the Ministry of Labour by not supporting: increasing the minimum wage; increasing the minimum daily amount for unemployment; the fixed amount for maximum daily unemployment benefit; increasing the monetary compensation for raising a child to two years, which does not stimulate the return to the labour market. ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-09.u362	Dear Mr. Chairman, honourable Members of the Parliament, Dear Mr. Minister, Dear Mr. Ivanov, Dear ladies and gentlemen Deputy Ministers! The Bulgarian legislation, which regulates the issues of birth and raising a child, has undergone a number of changes during the years of transition, while maintaining its main goal of stimulating the birth rate. In Bulgaria the period of maternity is for 2 years, and in the first year the mother receives 90% of her income before birth, and in the second year she receives monetary compensation for additional paid leave, the amount of which is determined each year in the State Social Security Budget Act. This legislative law gives Bulgaria one of the first places not only in Europe, but also in the world as regards the fact that the state guarantees one of the longest and longest motherhoods, which, however, is not a guarantee of an increase in birth rates and for overcoming the demographic crisis. During the years of GERB management we have kept this period unchanged because we believe that mothers should be given enough time to raise the child through a guaranteed by the state period during which to take care of it, but also the right to return to their place of employment before she has used all the additional leave, taking advantage of the envisaged opportunity in art. 54 of the Social Security Code shall receive 50% of the compensation foreseen for the second year. The proposed Draft Law on the Budget of State Social Security for 2022 provides for this compensation for the second year to be 710 BGN, and for the period from 1 April to 31 December 2022, i.e. this compensation to be equal to the amount of minimum wage. But will this amount be sufficient to encourage parents to have one or two more children, will this amount be sufficient to cover all the costs of families in terms of raising and providing the needs of the child in the second year of his life in a situation of galoping inflation and constantly rising prices of goods and services?! To these issues, of course, I am obliged to add the main problem that is related to the labour market, and it is related to the ageing of the workforce, the shortage of the workforce. And this, combined with the constantly changing requirements for qualification and professional skills of employees, requires the adoption of such policies that will stimulate the faster return of parents to the labour market. Obviously, however, in this budget, which is currently being proposed for consideration, we do not see such policies, but only this is being compensated by handing out money. If we allow the current provisions of the Social Security Code to operate, it means that we agree to passively receive financial support from the state, which will neither lead to an increase in birth rates nor to encourage them to return more quickly to the labour market. It is precisely everything that I have set out that motivates us all from the parliamentary group of GERB-SDS to make a proposal between first and second reading of the draft Law on the Budget of the State Social Security compensation for unused additional paid leave for raising a child up to two years of age to be 100% of the amount of compensation received by the mother in the second year of the child. This means that she will have the right to choose whether to return to work and receive 100% of the compensation for unused paid leave plus her salary, or to stay home to take care of her child until the age of two. Thank you. [[Applause from GREB-SDS.]]

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-09.u510	Ladies and gentlemen, please take your seats. Moving on to item three of the agenda: SELECTION OF A PRESIDENT AND ONE COMMISSION MEMBER FOR ENERGY AND WATER REGULATION. Before we move on to the Energy Commission Report, I will remind you of the Rules of Election of President and Member, as published in the Official Gazette, in the part concerning the work of the Chamber: 1. The meeting is broadcast in real time on the Internet through the specialized thematic website of the National Assembly. With the National Assembly's decision the proposed candidates are present at the meeting. 2. The National Assembly shall hear the report of the Energy Committee. 3. Each candidate shall be submitted by the importer within two minutes. 4. Discussions on applications shall be held in accordance with the procedure laid down in Article 1. 52 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly. 5. The National Assembly shall elect separately the Chair of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission and one member with experience in the energy sector of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission. 6. The vote is evident through the computerised voting system. If there are several candidates for one position, the vote shall be carried out in alphabetical order on the applicant's own names unless the National Assembly decides otherwise. 7. The candidate is considered to have received more than half of the votes of the present MPs. If several candidates for the respective position have received more than half of the votes of the present Members of the National Assembly, the candidate has been selected with the highest number of votes for the House. 8. Where none of the candidates for the position concerned receives the necessary votes, a re-voting shall take place, involving the two candidates who received the most votes for the position concerned. 9. If none of the candidates for the respective position receive the necessary votes in the second vote, the National Assembly shall adopt a decision to open a new selection procedure. 10. When one of the candidates refuses to participate in the election before the election of his candidacy by the National Assembly and he is the only candidate for office, the National Assembly shall adopt a decision to open a new procedure for the election of the Chair of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission or one member with experience in the energy sector of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission. 11. If there is no candidate for any of the positions, the National Assembly shall adopt a decision to open a new procedure for the election of this position. Please invite the Quaestors to the hall Stanislav Todorov and Blagoy Golubarev. A report from the Energy Commission on the hearing of candidates for Chairman and member of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission has come in. I give the floor to Mr. Ribarsky to present the report.

The sitting was broadcast in real time through the website of the National Assembly. At the beginning of the meeting, the members of the Energy Commission unanimously decided that, clearly from the documents submitted, candidates were eligible for the posts for which they were proposed and should be heard. For Chairman of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission Stanislav Todorov has been promoted. Mr. Stanislav Todorov, proposed and presented by the MP Kaloyan Ikonomov from the parliamentary group of the Parliamentary Group. He pointed out that Stanislav Todorov graduated law at Sofia University in 2001, after which he worked as a lawyer at the Ministry of Defence for three years. In 2006 he graduated from Master's degree in International Business Law in Budapest, Hungary, and then started working for leading foreign investors in the world of industry in Bulgaria, such as AES, Heidelberg Cement and Visteon Electronics. During his professional career he has led departments for trade contracts, supplies and logistics. He knows Bulgaria's electricity system well. After the launch of the Central into operation creates and manages the supply and logistics department in the town of Galabovo, where the Central is located. Since 2013 he has headed the AES Supply and Logistics Management Unit for the whole region of Europe - Asia, including the Central Office in Bulgaria for two years. In 2017 he started working at the Bulgarian factory of Heidelberg Cement in Devnya for three years as Director of Supply and Logistics for Bulgaria and Greece. As of January 18, 2022, he served as Deputy Minister of Energy of the Republic of Bulgaria with a portfolio of Energy Policies. On his proposal, a business with compressed natural gas is being developed and implemented, the so-called virtual gas pipeline. With his professional experience Mr. Golubarev has participated in the construction and licensing of a number of projects and objects in the field of electricity and natural gas. It has a comprehensive view of the processes in energy, both from the position of a civil servant and from the private sector; both from construction, repair, maintenance and exploitation, and in the process of trading electricity and gas. The candidates for Chairman and member of the KEWRC presented their concepts of the Commission's activities to the Energy Commission. Stanislav Todorov, a candidate for President of the Commission, outlined his concept, stressing that customers are at the centre of the work of the Commission not only in terms of maintaining bearable energy and water services prices, but also being an active participant in the transformation of the energy system through their own production and consumption management. Active work and coordination with consumer organizations, the Ministry of Energy, the Government and the National Assembly is needed to implement the energy transition and energy strategy of the Republic of Bulgaria in the most end-user-friendly way. When changing the Commission's focus, it can be an engine of market transformation and the role of consumers in the process of decarbonisation, the Green transaction and the decentralisation of energy. The Commission's role can be strengthened with regard to the development of a free wholesale and retail market for gas and electricity, actively exercising the powers under REMIT and the Energy Act. In the concept presented by Mr. Golubarev, the following are highlighted: The methodology for implementing REMIT is discussed, but with great delay and has not resulted in the desired result, which requires measures to be taken in this direction. In the case of a supplier last resort, the methodology for determining electricity prices from 2021 does not solve the problems in the sector, but deepens them, which leads to the need for its update. By 2025, domestic consumers are about to pass on the free market, which requires a strategy to avoid repeating the problem of non-domestic consumers. There is a need for a clear border between "Transition" and "Distribution" as there is currently a duplication of structures and activities leading to increased costs. For gas, market opening is necessary. At the moment it is a mix of............................................................................................... Competition would lead to a decrease in price. Serious work is needed to increase the confidence in the actions of the KEWRC. Publicity, transparency, discussion at all levels, not only in the regulated format. However, as you say, I have not done law for a long time, there are enough lawyers in this National Assembly. I'd like to say a few things so you can get to know me. I will share some things from my life and professional experience, as well as with regard to what I think the KEWRC should do in the next five years. I've worked in the public administration for three years. I've worked as a public procurement lawyer. The matter at least by then the applicable law knows it well, I know when the Law should be applied and when it should not be applied. After my brief stay in the public administration, I started working, as I said during the presentation, for leading investors. For the most part of my life and professional experience, I have worked for private companies and I have dealt with private contracts, not public. In all those positions I've worked on, I may have managed contracts worth over 300 million. Euros. A large part of these contracts have been concluded by me, those that have been higher than my limits, than the executives concerned. I've always responded directly to the CEO of the company I worked at. All this time, I've been using these people's trust for managing the money and the needs of the business. With regard to my experience in the electricity sector, I think there is no doubt that AES is one of the major energy enterprises in Bulgaria. I've managed absolutely all A.S. contracts as far as supplies are concerned, as regards the purchase of goods and services. I know very well how the price structure of such an enterprise is formed, I know very well what to look for and what to judge as part of the KEWRC. Because in a large part the powers of the KEWRC are expressed in assessing whether the price structure, costs and contracts that have been made can be recognised as such, so as to enter the price of the regulated market and be invoiced accordingly as a cost to our consumers. In this sense, I think my experience is very relevant and that the KEWRC will only benefit from one if you make the choice to be the chairman of the KEWRC. I realize that the responsibility is very great and I want to tell you a few thoughts on this subject. Prices in Bulgaria, as far as consumers and end customers are concerned, are determined by the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission. The popular thesis that the Minister of Energy can influence these prices is somewhat correct, and only to the extent that about 80% of the electricity in Bulgaria is produced by state-controlled sources. But the KEWRC is the regulator and the KEWRC is the one who has to help all of us, including consumers understand why prices are such and what they are formed of. Energy Minister, the government cannot reduce energy prices in Bulgaria. They are what they are, because Bulgaria is an open system, we are connected with both Greece and Macedonia and all neighbouring countries. Currently, Bulgaria exports about 1300 megawatts of energy to neighbouring markets. Although Bulgarian plants produce at low cost, as the price of coal and production is low, they manage to reap good profits, in my opinion, by producing and exporting their energy outside. And there's nothing wrong with that. Bad comes the moment we have such over-concentration of supply. Agree that when we are talking about a free market in electricity, it is difficult to talk about a free market when a market participant, and this is the state, formally controls 80% of supply. And here is the role of the Regulator to create such rules so that the Bulgarian business can benefit from the fact that Bulgaria has a very well developed structure of energy production at a very low price. That's not the case right now. And I personally see the role of the Regulator precisely in this regard.. to begin to expect from market participants behavior that corresponds to their position. When it is a trading company, it should take care of its profits; when it is a public supplier, it should take care of achieving a maximum low price for consumers. It is the Regulator with his regulations and policy that must make this transformation. We have a very big change ahead of us - whether we want to or not - is not up to us. The entire energy structure of Europe and the whole world is changing and will change in the next five years. If we constantly think through the prism of what we have at the moment and how to protect the most existing economic and political interests, we will crash. Because this effort to move on to an economy that is not based on coal, which is open to every consumer and consumer at the same time for not only solar parks to be built by people who are well-connected with power in this or the next government, but for everyone to be able to put panels on their roof, it depends on the regulator. The regulator can create conditions in which to maximize the cost of this transition for the Bulgarian consumer. And yes, I believe that there is something to change in the work of the Regulator, although I personally respect Mr. Ivan Ivanov and have always respected him as an expert and as a leader. That's what I was able to tell him today when we met. But there's something to change. I would like to change that the KEWRC is not a leader of market participants, but to be an engine of change by providing information, by adopting new rules and changing these rules when necessary. So, all together, the executive, legislative power and the regulator, to direct market participants in the direction that will help us move into this new model of energy and economics. I am aware that what I am telling you is certainly not going to satisfy everyone, but I will only remind you that I often say: while preparing for the hearing in the Commission, I saw exactly what was going on in the election of Mr. Ivan Ivanov and I came across the following article: After scandals and debates, the new chairman of the KEWRC elected. This is not new and I realize that it is part of political life. Therefore, I will not make specific answers to specific statements by you, Members of the National Assembly. I just wanted to get out on the stand so you could see what kind of person stands and give you some confidence, if I can, that the regulation of the market is in the hands of both gas and water supply, sewerage, electricity and heat supply. Thank you. [[Applause from "We continue the Change", "BSP for Bulgaria", INN and SG.]]

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-09.u641	Thank you. ♪ I can't believe on a bill amending and supplementing the Corporate Income Tax Act, No 47-254-01-11, submitted by the National Representative Lyubomir Karimanski and a group of MPs on 26 January 2022. At a meeting held on 27 January 2022, the Budget and Finance Committee examined that bill. The meeting was attended by the representatives of the Ministry of Finance Mr. Ivaylo Yaydzhiev Deputy Minister, Mrs. Ludmila Petkova Director of the Department of Tax Policy and Mrs. Petya Kopcheva Head of the Department of Tax Policy Directorate and representatives of the National Association of Municipalities in the Republic of Bulgaria Ms. Silvia Georgieva Executive Director, Ms. Daniela Usatova and Ms. Desislava Stojkova, as well as representatives of employers' and professional organizations. The bill was presented by Mr. Lyubomir Karimanski. The bill cancels the tax on costs in kind relating to own, employed and/or assets provided for personal use and/or personnel use. It is proposed that, as of 1 January 2023, these costs be treated for tax purposes as unrecognized costs, which preserves the concept that costs that are not related to the taxable person's business are not recognised for tax purposes through the relevant tax technique. In order to reduce the administrative burden, and due to the need to find an application-enhanced regime that does not burden natural persons, it is proposed that natural persons who have received non-cash income, who are not recognised for tax purposes for the employer, costs relating to the use of assets and/or personnel of the company for private purposes, be exempt from taxation under the Law on Income Tax on Natural Persons. The Law introduces a transitional norm related to food vouchers. It is proposed in 2022 that employers provide the food vouchers due but not provided, together with the vouchers for next month. In such cases, it is proposed that the total amount of non-taxable food vouchers provided be up to the legally established non-taxable amount for each of the months covered by the vouchers provided for each employee. In the Transitional and Final Provisions of the Bill, amendments to other laws are proposed as follows: The law on personal income taxes provides that interest income on deposits in banks and branches of banks, where both banks and branches of banks are established in a Member State of the European Union or in another State, a party to the Agreement on the European Economic Area, should be included in the scope of non-taxable income under the Law. Under the Law on Local Taxes and Fees (ZMDT) it is proposed to drop the fees for kindergartens and kindergartens and to make use of these kindergartens free of charge for parents. The aim of the proposal is to relieve the parents of the children in nursery and gardens. As regards the proposed amendments to the ZMDT, amendments to the Pre-school and School Education Act and the Health Act are also proposed. In the Transitional and Final Provisions of the Law amending and supplementing the Tax and Insurance Procedure Code (prom., SG No. 105 of 2020, p. 23 from 2021) the creation of a special provision ensuring the possibility of monthly collateral and charges for 2021 under art. 60, para. 2, p. Art. 1 and 2 and art. 64, para. 1 of the Waste Management Act to remain at the expense of the municipality which collected them and to be spent by decision of the municipal council through internal offset changes, without amending the method adopted by the municipal council to determine the amount of the municipal waste charge. It is also proposed that, in the current 2022, these own funds of municipalities should not be blocked on the accounts of the Regional Environmental and Water Inspection, but that they may be used by decision of the municipal councils for waste management activities. The bill complies with the requirements of the Law on Regulatory Acts, the Decree on its implementation and of the Rules on Organization and Activity of the National Assembly. In their written opinions the representatives of the Ministry of Finance, NSORB and BCCI support the bill. The Association of Municipalities considers that legal and editorial amendments to some of the texts should be carried out between the first and second votes in order to achieve clarity and full coverage of the agreed priorities in the coalition agreement and to synchronise the provisions with the adoption of the CCB for 2022. KT "Support" supports the proposals for dropping the fees for kindergartens and nurseries, and with regard to the tax, they offer to stay for deposits over 100 000 BGN. and it shall be flattened as a minimum with the tax on income from labour. BCCI supports the initiative to limit the so-called "Wickend" tax, as well as dropping fees in nursery and gardens. During the discussion Mr. Krasimir Valchev expressed satisfaction that the fees for kindergartens and nurseries were dropped. People's representative Bajram Bajram pointed out that their party's programme also includes dropping fees for nursery and gardens, interest tax and expressing support for municipalities. On the other proposals, he believes that there should be more quantitative indicators in order to assess what the impact on the budget will be. During the public discussion the representative of BIA Mr. Stanislav Popdonchev emphasized that they admire the dropping of the tax on interest rates, as well as the prescribed provisions for food vouchers. He took note of a change in the regime of the so-called "wickend' tax system, explaining that the tax burden from one tax to another is being transferred, which will inevitably aggravate the situation of some tax entities. It proposed to consider maintaining the existing regime and thresholds or reducing the number of assets falling within the personal use. Mr. Iliya Iliev from IDES supported the position of BIA and pointed out that it is necessary to clarify the texts regarding the tax "Wickend" in order to assess what is the purpose of the He said that many companies are currently working the so-called home office and their employees are using their personal resources to do their work. Mr. Lyubomir Karimanski stressed that we should consider what is the best mechanism and balance. He said he would make a proposal between the first and second voting of the Law on an optimal option concerning the regime of the so-called tax. Mr. Dobrin Ivanov of AICB believes that the problem with the cancellation of fees for kindergartens and nurseries is not at the expensive price, but in the lack of accessibility and places in them. Supports the dropping of the interest tax, as well as the proposal relating to municipalities. He added his colleagues with regard to the tax, in the sense that if it falls, the situation of the employees will deteriorate and the personal use will be expensive. The Executive Director of NSORB Ms. Silvia Georgieva said that from a fee for household waste around 630 million are collected. BGN and between 150 million And 180 million. lv. are the calculations under the Waste Management Act, which are blocked in foreign accounts each year and if they are released, municipalities will be able to refinance their accounts plan. As regards the fees for nurseries and gardens, she stressed that for 2020, municipalities and parents financed 250 million. BGN 192 million lv. is own revenue and 58 million. lv. are taxes collected by the parents. During the discussion Mr. Ivaylo Dermenjiev took part in the discussion. He expressed the position of the lawyer to increase the statutory expenses for lawyers from 25% to 40%. According to the lawyers' estimates, the actual expenses incurred are of the order of 70%. He explained that it is about the income of about 10,000 lawyers, most of them receiving around BGN 1,700. a month, and if their offer is accepted, they'll be in the treasury by 3 million. lv. Each year, less income from taxes, but tax collection will increase. Lawyer Petar Slavov added that lately the expenses of lawyers for their activities have increased. Mr. Lyubomir Karimanski summarized that concrete evidence is needed as to how collection will be improved, as well as specific data on the need to increase regulatoryly recognised costs. Mr. Rumen Gechev said that it is a necessary expert assessment and also to find a balance of public interest. Mrs. Venice Angova asked for more information about how direct costs are valued and about their sources, to which Mr. Ivaylo Dermendzhiev replied that he has indicated approximate numbers taken by the 28 law colleagues in the country. Ms. Ludmila Petkova argues the position of the Ministry of Finance on the issue of increasing the statutory costs for lawyers. She pointed out that the 25 per cent expenditure was adopted in 2007 when Bulgaria joined the European Union and this preference was assessed. Point out that the debate on statutoryly recognised costs for lawyers will also open a debate on other free professions and farmers. In addition, with a different treatment of one kind of income, this will raise the question whether it is not State aid because a preference for a particular circle of persons has been defined. After the discussion was concluded, a vote was held, which received the following results: 15 votes for the debate, without the opposition and without the abstention. On the basis of the above and the results of the vote, the Committee on Budget and Finance proposes to the National Assembly to adopt, in the first vote, a bill amending and supplementing the Corporate Income Tax Act, No 47-254-01-11, submitted by the National Representative Lyubomir Karimanski and a group of MPs on 26 January 2022.
